| Technical spec: Section 13 Shared Workstations | In progress | [M3.8](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M2.6](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M16.8](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M16.9](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `SharedWorkstationSchemaTest`; `SharedWorkstationLoginCodeTest`; `SharedWorkstationLoginCodeOrchidTest`; `SharedWorkstationSessionTest`; `apps/client/src/session/workstationSession.spec.ts`; `apps/client/src/views/KioskSessionViews.spec.ts` | [QA-AUTH-01](../qa/QA-AUTH-01-email-magic-link-login.md) | Shared workstation and human-typable login-code records exist (M3.8): trusted/revoked workstation state, device-backed trust checks, pinned Kiosk organization/event/optional-department context, context-pinned metadata, six-week login-code expiry, used/revoked code state, and hashed-code storage only. M16.8 implements the section 13.2 login-code lifecycle on top of them: generation by God mode for any known user and by a user for themselves, keyed hashing with no raw code persisted, generation rate limiting per user and per node, per-workstation entry-attempt limiting, God Mode listing and revocation, and audit on generation, use, and failed entry past a threshold. Codes stay scoped to one user, one event taken from the workstation's pinned context, and one trusted workstation, and are not printable or exportable — the console shows a code once, in the render that created it, and stores only its hash. Code entry is a domain service rather than an endpoint: AUTH-029's per-workstation limiting and 13.2's minimal use audit are properties of the code, while what a successful entry establishes is section 13.3. M16.9 implements 13.3. The 5 minutes are the node's: expiry is derived from `last_activity_at` rather than stamped, every authenticated request slides it, and a session observed past its window is ended and stamped at the moment it expired rather than the moment it was noticed. Lock on Electron restart is a property of the credential rather than a behavior of the renderer — the session key is returned once, held in memory only, and never written to disk, and a workstation session's `/api/me` document is deliberately not cached either, because a session document on disk is how the previous user comes back on screen for whoever restarts the machine. "Users must explicitly end their session before switching users" is enforced where switching happens, in the Kiosk shell and its router, not as a node-side refusal to start a second session: a Kiosk that crashed still holds a session it can no longer present, and refusing would have locked the workstation out for up to five minutes at the moment somebody needs it, so starting supersedes and records that it did. "Permissions come entirely from the active user" holds because a resolved session produces a user and nothing else, and a user disabled after signing in resolves to nobody; the pinned workstation context frames the shell and grants no authority. Ending wipes session data through the same context-reset registry an event switch runs, which keeps anything still pending sync, so queued operations survive an end and a timeout both. The kiosk guide's warning before the timeout is implemented as part of the session bar, with continuing routed through the session read so saying "I'm still here" is itself the activity that slides the window. `kiosk.switch-user`, `kiosk.reauth`, `kiosk.shift-board`, and the setup/support surfaces remain their own tasks. |
| Data/API spec: Section 12.3 `shared_workstations`; Section 12.4 `shared_workstation_login_codes`; Section 12.6 `shared_workstation_sessions` | In progress | [M3.8](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M2.6](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M16.8](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M16.9](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `SharedWorkstationSchemaTest`; `SharedWorkstationLoginCodeTest`; `SharedWorkstationLoginCodeOrchidTest`; `SharedWorkstationSessionTest` | [QA-AUTH-01](../qa/QA-AUTH-01-email-magic-link-login.md) | `shared_workstations` and `shared_workstation_login_codes` tables/models are present with UUID primary keys, device/workstation/user/generator relationships, pinned organization/event/optional-department context, trusted/revoked state, six-week expiry, used/revoked timestamps, and `code_hash` without raw login-code persistence. M16.8 closes the 12.4 rules other than the session an entry establishes: generation and use are audited, raw codes are never logged, codes are scoped to one user/event/trusted workstation, `generated_by_user_id` records the operator on the God Mode path and equals `user_id` on the self-service path, generation is rate limited per user and per node, entry attempts are rate limited per workstation with failures audited past a threshold, and a successful entry issues no API token under 12.5. The stored hash is HMAC keyed to the application key and namespaced away from the `api_login_codes` hash, so the same eight characters do not produce the same digest in both tables and a hash cannot be carried between the two credentials. A new code for the same user and workstation retires the one they held, so a forgotten code does not leave a second six-week credential live — the same rule the mailed API login code follows. Formal foreign keys for organization/event/department context remain deferred because the original shared-workstation migration predates those domain migrations, which is why generation requires the pinned event to resolve to an event this node holds rather than trusting the column. M16.9 adds section 12.6 and the table it names: `shared_workstation_sessions`, carrying the workstation, user, event, the code it came from, a keyed hash of the session key, `started_at`, `last_activity_at`, and `ended_at` with a reason of `signed_out`, `timed_out`, or `superseded`. It is a separate table from 12.5 rather than a token with a flag, because AUTH-030 says a code entry issues no token and AUTH-024 makes the two lifetimes independent — a shared column is how something eventually treats one as the other. Expiry is derived from `last_activity_at` rather than stored, so the 5-minute rule in 12.3 has exactly one owner and no second copy to drift from it. `event_id` comes from the code, which took it from the workstation's pinned context, and never from a request; it carries no foreign key for the same deferred reason the code's does not. Session ends are audited as `shared_workstation_session.ended` with the reason, which is the part the code's `used` entry does not already record. |

Team-membership-driven authority and explainable role resolution are present, including event/team-scoped IC grants constrained to the selected IC department. M11.4 applies that model to online incident creation for `ic_lead` and `ic_operator`. M11.5 applies it to restricted incident list/detail reads and restricted UI messaging for non-IC actors. M11.12 grants organizers and lead organizers `organization.departments.manage` for organization-scoped department administration. M11.14 grants organizers and lead organizers `organization.staff.manage` for organization-scoped staff intake/lead selection, and department lead selection still flows through team membership plus `team_grants`. M11.13 grants department-scoped `department.administer` to `department_lead` and `department_administration` for permitted department details and team lifecycle administration. M11.16 grants `department.trainings.manage` to `department_lead`/`department_administration` (department-scoped) and organizer roles (organization-scoped) for product training management, with team leads authorized as trainers for their team's trainings. The catalog now has a read-only God Mode console surface listing every effective role with its scope and capabilities, every registered capability with the roles that carry it, and any drift between what a deployment has stored and what the build defines. It is read-only because the catalog is a property of the build: a deployment that could edit it would enforce permissions differently from every other node running the same code. The console's own role list, which is the administrative framework's and governs console access only, is relabelled Console Roles so the two are not mistaken for each other. | Data/API spec: Section 14.1 `audit_events` | In progress | [M4.9](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `AuditEventSchemaTest`; `AuditServiceTest` |  | `audit_events` table and immutable `AuditEvent` model are present with a UUID primary key (per the section 4.1 identifier policy), nullable organization/event/department scope, nullable actor user/device/node references (restrict-on-delete to preserve history), action, entity type/ID, before/after JSON, reason, source context, signature metadata JSON, and a single `created_at` timestamp. Model guards reject updates and deletes. The foreign-key columns and polymorphic `entity_id` are now UUID following the canonical-key remediation ([005](../issues/005-uuid-primary-key-remediation.md)). `sync_conflicts` (14.2) is delivered by M12.8. |
| Technical spec: Section 10.3 Conflict policy; Data/API spec: Section 7.5 Sync Conflicts; Section 14.2 `sync_conflicts`; UI Implementation Contract: `orchid.sync-conflicts` | In progress | [M12.8](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M12.9](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `SyncConflictSchemaTest`; `SyncConflictServiceTest`; `SyncConflictResolverTest`; `SyncConflictOrchidTest`; `NodeOperationReceiveApplyTest` | [QA-SYNC-01](../qa/QA-SYNC-01-onsite-central-sync.md) | M12.8 creates the God-mode sync conflict queue. `sync_conflicts` stores the documented section 14.2 fields with a UUID primary key, FK to `node_operations`, local/remote JSON snapshots, reason, open/resolved status, and nullable review/resolution columns (`accept_onsite` / `accept_central` matching technical spec 10.3). `SyncConflictService::record` inserts an open conflict and marks the related operation `conflicted` in one transaction. Appliers signal disagreements with `SyncConflictException`; `NodeOperationReceiver::apply` records the queue row instead of a retriable failure, skips re-applying conflicted operations, and continues applying unrelated operations so unresolved conflicts do not block sync. God Mode Orchid list/detail screens (`platform.sync-conflicts`) are permission-gated, ordered by entity type, and show local/remote values. M12.9 adds the resolver. `SyncConflictResolver` decides which of the two stored versions a choice names from node roles rather than from wire direction — the central side is whichever of the two nodes holds the `central` role and the other side is the on-site side — so accept on-site applies the arriving operation on central and keeps local state on the on-site node, and accept central is the mirror of that. Accepting the remote version is the only path that re-applies a conflicted operation: `NodeOperationReceiver::applyResolvedConflict()` clears the conflicted mark and re-runs the applier with `SignedNodeOperation::$resolvedConflict` set, which tells an applier that the disagreement it raised has been reviewed and must be overwritten rather than raised again; an applier that raises it anyway is marked `failed` instead of queueing a second row for a decided disagreement. Applying runs before the conflict row is closed, so a resolution that could not write the accepted version leaves the conflict open and unaudited rather than claiming the disagreement was settled. Accepting the local version writes no entity state and leaves the operation `conflicted`, because it was received and never applied; operations are append-only, so only `failure_reason` is annotated to say a reviewer superseded it. Values are never edited in the resolver (technical spec 10.3): both versions stay read-only and the only writes are to one of the two versions already shown. The recommended default follows technical spec 10.3 — event-scoped records inside an active event window default to accept on-site, central and global records default to accept central — and is displayed rather than applied, because the specification has a human choose. Resolution is audited as `sync_conflict.resolved` in the same transaction that closes the conflict, capturing the reviewer, before/after status and resolution, the accepted side and value, the operation uuid and resulting status, and the event when the operation names one. A resolved conflict cannot be resolved again, an unknown resolution is refused, and a conflict between two nodes on the same side of the central split is refused rather than decided on the reviewer's behalf. Electron severe-conflict health is M12.10; the human QA script is M12.11; attention-list linking is M15B.5. |

Decisions notify the submitter (VOL-025) and every change is audited with before and after values (VOL-026). `staff_profile_change_requests` is specified in data/API section 10.4 and does not exist. | Data/API spec: Section 10.8 `trainings`, `training_prerequisites`, `training_signups`, `training_completions` | In progress | [M7.1](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M11.16](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M16.16](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `TrainingSchemaTest`; `TrainingDomainTest`; `TrainingAdminHttpTest`; `DepartmentTrainingViews.spec.ts` | [QA-TRAIN-01](../qa/QA-TRAIN-01-training-management.md) | `trainings`, `training_prerequisites`, and `training_completions` tables/models/factories are present with UUID keys, nullable organization-wide/department/team/event scope, `expires_after_days`, completion `completed_at`/`expires_at`/`recorded_by_user_id`/`origin_node_id`, unique prerequisite pairs, and `TrainingService` domain logic for prerequisite graph integrity and completion-expiry derivation. M11.16 adds delivery fields (`delivery`/`online_url`), scheduled-attendance fields (`scheduled_start_at`/`scheduled_end_at`/`location`/`capacity`), training-page fields (`time_commitment`/`after_training`/`provisions`), and `linked_shift_id` on `trainings`; the `training_signups` roster table with one row per training/staff pair and recorded (not deleted) cancellation; linked-shift materialization for event-bound in-person scheduled trainings whose signups flow through normal shift signup with prerequisites registered as shift training requirements; the `department.trainings.manage` permission; product API commands/reads under `/api`; and audit wiring for create/update/archive/prerequisite/signup/completion/import actions. M16.16 makes those reads what the client surfaces render from, and adds the facts they needed to be sufficient on their own: `access.can_manage` / `access.can_record_completions` on the department read state the caller's authority over the surface as a whole, which a per-training `viewer` block cannot answer for a department with no trainings yet; the read also carries the department's teams and its active roster, each only when the caller's authority entitles them to it; and every prerequisite entry carries `viewer_completed`, so "before you start" distinguishes what is done from what is left. `GET /api/departments/{department}/trainings/{training}` refuses a training in another department, and an archived training read by a member, with a stated `Training not found for this department.` message rather than a bare 404. Orchid repair screens, OpenAPI, sync projections, and shift eligibility enforcement remain with their owning tasks. |
| TRAIN-001 | In progress | [M7.1](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M11.16](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M16.16](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `TrainingSchemaTest`; `TrainingAdminHttpTest`; `DepartmentTrainingViews.spec.ts` | [QA-TRAIN-01](../qa/QA-TRAIN-01-training-management.md) | Trainings model event-specific (nullable `event_id`), annual (`expires_after_days`), and one-off (no event/no expiry) variants through scope and expiry fields rather than a separate type enum, per data/API section 10.8. M11.16 adds the normal Meridian Admin product UI and API for department/organizer training creation and maintenance (`department.trainings` route, `TrainingAdminService`, `TrainingProductAccess`), including scheduled-session, team-scope, location, and capacity setup. M16.16 binds that UI to those endpoints: the list and the create/edit form read and write the node's trainings, the team-scope field offers the teams the read carried, and whether a training takes signups follows the node's `requires_scheduled_attendance` rather than the client's reading of the start time. |

M7.7 adds `ShiftOverlapService`, `Shift::overlaps`, advisory overlap warnings on self-signup, and `ShiftAssignmentService::assignStaffToShift` for department-lead assignment with elevated overlap authority. M7.8 adds schedule lock configuration and enforcement plus lead/self removal services. M7.9 recalculates event credential state after signup, assignment, and removal. M10.7 adds `UnscheduledShiftAdditionService` so Department Logistics can add on-site eligible staff to a started shift without reopening planned signup, with overlap warnings, audit, and credential recalculation that preserves CRED-014 non-counting for after-start unscheduled assignments. API/OpenAPI, Orchid screens, and sync projections remain deferred to their owning tasks. M7.11 and M10.7 add human QA coverage. M16.18 makes `GET /api/departments/{department}/shifts` the one read the client's shift surfaces render from, and adds the facts they needed to be sufficient on their own: `access.can_manage` states the caller's authority over the surface as a whole, which a department with no shifts yet still has to answer for; `can_manage` on each shift states it for that row, so the actions offered are the ones the commands would accept; and `teams` narrowed from every department team to the active teams this caller may schedule for, so the eligible-team field cannot propose a team the command would refuse. The read also answers a department member with active team membership, read-only, with the active shifts their teams are eligible for and no option lists at all — that is the Staff menu's Shifts entry, and it was previously served from bundled fixture data. |

The set is unchanged; the client and the node now read it from one place. `POST /api/commands/revoke-credential` refuses an unauthorized caller with 403 and a staff member with no credential or shift history with 422, and both endpoints of the `organizer.credentials` surface carry the same capability — so a department lead who may export the same event's eligibility (REPORT-007) is refused here. |

| SLB-015 through SLB-018 | Complete | [M10.1A](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M10.9B](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M16.21](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M18.1](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `DepartmentPresenceTest`; `DepartmentOperationsCommandHttpTest`; `DepartmentOperationsReadHttpTest`; `DepartmentOpsViews.spec.ts`; `PermissionCatalogTest` | [QA-SLB-01](../qa/QA-SLB-01-checkin-checkout-hours.md); [Department operations UX smoke](../qa/README.md#alpha-1-department-operations-ux-smoke) | `event_department_presences` tracks on-site and off-site per event, department, and staff member, and `DepartmentPresenceService` has refused an off-site move while somebody is checked in or holding equipment since M10.4. Until M16.21 nothing could reach it: presence had no endpoint, so the Logistics Window two buttons moved a value in the browser and no record anywhere. `POST /api/commands/mark-staff-on-site` and `POST /api/commands/mark-staff-off-site` are the transport, `department.presence.manage` is the authority, and the desk shows the service own refusal sentence — the read carries it on each workspace as `off_site_blocked_reason`, so the operator reads why before pressing anything. M18.1 is the task these two commands and their controls belong to; M16.21 built them early because the Logistics Desk could not be bound to real staff without them, so what M18.1 closes is the one place the desk and the command still disagreed. SLB-018 blocks an off-site mark on held equipment "unless the equipment is returned or marked Missing/Damaged", and the exception was only in the service: the read counted every unreturned checkout, so an item written off from God Mode while its checkout was open showed a refusal sentence the command would not have refused with. Both now ask `EquipmentCheckout::blocksOffSite()` over the same `outstandingForDepartment` scope, and the item stays in the workspace's equipment list either way, because the department is still owed it. The equipment half of the block also gained the coverage the checked-in half already had, at the command, the read, and the desk. |
| SLB-019 | Complete | [M10.9A](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M10.1B](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M16.21](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `DepartmentOperationsReadHttpTest`; `departmentOpsReadModel.spec.ts`; `DepartmentOpsViews.spec.ts` | [QA-SLB-01](../qa/QA-SLB-01-checkin-checkout-hours.md); [Department operations UX smoke](../qa/README.md#alpha-1-department-operations-ux-smoke) | The Planning Table renders identity-free plan-versus-actual aggregate rows by shift and team window: capacity or No target, signed-up-or-assigned, checked-in, no-show, unscheduled additions, planned hours, actual hours, and variance with a status label. The shift drill-down that used to list who was scheduled is gone with the fixture it read from: it named individual staff on the one surface this requirement says must not, and what a selected shift shows now is its own counts. |

| SLB-021 | Complete | [M10.9B](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M10.1B](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M16.21](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M18.8](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `DepartmentOperationsReadHttpTest`; `departmentOpsReadModel.spec.ts`; `DepartmentOpsViews.spec.ts` | [QA-SLB-01](../qa/QA-SLB-01-checkin-checkout-hours.md); [Department operations UX smoke](../qa/README.md#alpha-1-department-operations-ux-smoke) | The desk searches staff, equipment, and shifts for the current event and department. M16.21 makes that index the node: one read returns the department staff, its equipment with each item holder, and the shifts inside the desk twelve-hours-back, thirty-six-hours-forward horizon, and the search runs over what came back rather than over a request per lookup — which is why it is one read and not the search-plus-detail pair data/API 5.1 described before. M18.8 closes the offline half by making that one read durable: `logisticsDeskCache` stores the response on the device and `readLogisticsDesk` opens on the stored copy when the node cannot be reached, so a desk reopened out of coverage still looks somebody up and still checks them in. It discloses which copy it is searching and when that copy was taken rather than presenting a stale index as current. The fallback turns on the node not answering, not on it answering no: a refusal is served as a refusal, so a client never re-grants what the node withdrew (CLIENT-006, CLIENT-010). One department at a time, dropped on a context switch, a sign-out, and a shared-workstation session end (CLIENT-014). M18.9 generalises it. Technical spec 9.3 — "the device should cache as much authorized data as possible; offline data may be stale, but stale authorized data is better than no data" — asks every role's device to hold its authorized reads, and names them: a staff member's own shifts, department and team info, basic event info, published policies; a lead's Overview summaries, roster, schedule, and attendance; a planner's aggregate rows. M18.8 read SLB-021 as the only offline-read requirement and held the desk alone, so every other surface answered an unreachable node with "check the connection to this node". `readCache` now holds reads at the request seam and the desk's bespoke store is deleted, so the desk's offline search is one case of the general rule rather than an exception to it. |

Since M16.21 none of those come from a compiled-in capability record, and the shell still grants nothing. |

The times are pre-filled rather than blank because a correction is an edit to two numbers already on file, and a field the operator does not touch has to send back the moment it was filled from — which is why the desk builds its `datetime-local` values from local components rather than from `toISOString().slice(0, 16)`, the UTC wall time wearing a local label. Once `hours_worked.frozen_at` is set the card offers nothing and prints `HoursCorrectionService`'s own refusal, which names the moment the grace period closed in the event's time zone rather than stating that a grace period exists; the same sentence reaches the desk on `correct_hours_blocked_reason` and comes back from the command as a 422, so the screen and the node say one thing. A shift somebody was assigned to that ended with no hours at all reads that the check-out is what is missing, because that is the action available. |

The check-out operation that produced the record is still there beside it afterwards; nothing in this path rewrites or removes an operation already accepted. The audit entry carries the prior actual times, minutes, and attendance timestamps as well as the new ones, so a corrected record shows what it was as well as what it became and who moved it. Repeating an `operation_uuid` is the same correction and applies once. |
| EQUIP-001 through EQUIP-007 | In progress | [M10.9](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M11.18](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M16.17](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `EquipmentWorkflowTest`; `EquipmentInventoryHttpTest`; `departmentOpsReadModel.spec.ts`; `DepartmentOpsViews.spec.ts`; `DepartmentEquipmentViews.spec.ts`; `EquipmentOrchidTest` | [QA-EQUIP-01](../qa/QA-EQUIP-01-equipment-inventory-setup.md); [Department operations UX smoke](../qa/README.md#alpha-1-department-operations-ux-smoke) | Manual MVP equipment tracking is represented by canonical item states (`available`, `checked_out`, `returned`, `missing`, `damaged`), individual-staff checkout records, Department Logistics connected commands, audit history, Logistics Desk staff-workspace controls, and Orchid inventory visibility. M11.18 adds the normal product path that creates the inventory those workflows consume: the `department.equipment` page and embeddable featureset, `GET /api/departments/{department}/equipment`, and the `create-equipment-item`, `update-equipment-item`, `archive-equipment-item`, `restore-equipment-item`, and `import-equipment-inventory` commands, gated by `EquipmentInventoryAccess` on `department.equipment.manage` or `department.administer` within the owning department. `EquipmentInventoryService` keeps setup and operations separate: new equipment is always created Available, setup may set only Available/Missing/Damaged, and state changes and archiving are refused while an item has an open checkout, so Checked out and Returned stay products of check-out/check-in. Asset tags are unique within a department, which makes the CSV import (required `name` header; optional `asset_tag`/`serial_number`; per-row imported/skipped results) re-runnable without duplicating equipment. Archiving is soft and preserves checkout history. Equipment with no department stays Orchid repair tooling, and no product command accepts another department, so department-to-department allotments remain excluded by EQUIP-006 unless that requirement changes. M16.17 connects that path to the client: the page reads its department, its state options, its event scopes, and its rows from the one endpoint and re-reads after every write, so the inventory a maintainer builds is the inventory the Logistics Window will find rather than a bundled fixture that agreed with it by construction (CLIENT-023). The read stopped reporting an open checkout as a boolean and now carries the checkout — holder, time, and shift — because "no state change, no archive while checked out" is only a workable rule if the row says who is holding the item and which shift it went out on. Equipment checkout and return themselves are still fixture-driven: the Logistics Window has no read endpoint and offers fixture staff identifiers, so `checkout-equipment` and `return-equipment` are bound by M16.21 alongside attendance, and until then EQUIP-002 through EQUIP-004 are proven on the server by `EquipmentWorkflowTest` rather than end to end. Full custody chains (EQUIP-007), inventory ownership/allotments, serial-numbered policy, and durable offline equipment write sync remain deferred. Every item is one physical unit today, and the checkout dialog renders one checkbox per available unit — workable for a small inventory and not for a large one, which is what EQUIP-010 through EQUIP-017 and M18.24B through M18.24D address. |
| EQUIP-010 through EQUIP-017 | Not started | [M18.24B](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M18.24C](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M18.24D](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | Pending pooled equipment and equipment lookup tests |  | Equipment splits into individually tracked units, identified by asset tag or serial number, and pooled kinds held as a quantity on one record (EQUIP-010, EQUIP-011). Checkout finds tracked units by entering, scanning, or searching name, asset tag, or serial number rather than listing every unit, with an exact identifier match resolving directly so a handheld scanner acting as a keyboard completes a handoff, and an ambiguous or unmatched value reported rather than guessed (EQUIP-012, EQUIP-013). Pooled kinds are presented as a short quantity list (EQUIP-014). Lookup is scoped to the operator's authorized department and event, discloses nothing outside it, and resolves against the inventory the surface already holds so it works offline (EQUIP-015). Pooled availability is total quantity less open checkout quantity, derived rather than stored, and a pool is never stored `checked_out` (EQUIP-016). Pooled returns record quantity and condition, and missing or damaged units reduce the pool's serviceable quantity through an audited adjustment (EQUIP-017). `equipment_items.tracking`, `equipment_items.quantity_total`, `equipment_checkouts.quantity`, and `equipment_checkouts.quantity_returned` are specified in data/API section 10.13 and do not exist. Camera-based scanning and asset tag generation are out of scope for the milestone. |

| HOURS-008 | Complete | [M10.6](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M10.11](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M13.4](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M18.4](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `HoursCorrectionTest`; `AttendanceCommandHttpTest`; `DepartmentOperationsReadHttpTest`; `DepartmentOpsViews.spec.ts` | [QA-SLB-01](../qa/QA-SLB-01-checkin-checkout-hours.md) | `HoursCorrectionService::freezeHours` stamps `hours_worked.frozen_at`, audits the freeze, treats repeat freeze attempts idempotently, and `correctHours` rejects frozen records. M13.4 reports `correction_state` (`open` or `frozen`) and `frozen_at` on every exported row, so a reader can tell a final total from one that may still move before credits are calculated. M13.5 makes `frozen_at` the operative signal for credit calculation: an event holding any unfrozen hours record cannot be credited at all. M18.4 makes the freeze visible where somebody meets it: the desk's shift card withdraws **Correct hours** and prints the service's refusal, which now names the moment the grace period closed in the event's time zone rather than stating that a grace period exists — an operator an hour late and one a month late need different next moves, and only the date distinguishes them. Grace-period duration/configuration remains deferred to M18.14, which adds the configured period as a second gate rather than a replacement for this one; until it lands, `frozen_at` is the only date the product can report, and it is reported rather than paraphrased. |

| CREDIT-004 | Complete | [M13.5](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `CreditCalculationTest`; `CreditSchemaTest` |  | Entries are written frozen: calculation and freeze are the same moment, because the hours behind an entry can no longer move when it is written. `CreditLedgerEntry` refuses updates and deletes of a frozen row, a unique index on (`hours_worked_id`, `entry_type`) refuses a second calculated entry for the same hours, and a re-run counts existing entries and leaves them alone. A policy renamed or re-rated afterwards does not reprice frozen entries, which carry the name and multiplier they were calculated at in `calculation_basis`. |
| CREDIT-005 | Complete | [M13.5](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M13.6](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M13.9](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `CreditsEarnedExportTest`; `CreditCalculationTest` | [QA-EXPORT-01](../qa/QA-EXPORT-01-alpha-1-exports.md) | M13.5 froze the basis onto each ledger entry; M13.6 exports it. Every row of the credits earned export reports the minutes worked, the hours they rounded to, the policy name and multiplier they were priced at, the resulting credits, the policy source, the calculation moment, and the hours record's freeze and correction moments, so the arithmetic is re-checkable inside the row. The basis columns are read from the entry's frozen `calculation_basis` and never from the policy record it names, so a policy renamed or re-rated afterwards does not restate what an event already paid; a test renames and re-rates the organization default and asserts the exported name, multiplier, and credits are unchanged. A basis missing a key exports an empty cell rather than a substituted live value. |

Applicant emails matching existing organization-scoped DNS staff status are now recorded as `auto_rejected_dns` without applicant-facing DNS/rejection notice (M5.4). Orchid application review list/detail lets organizers inspect submitted and DNS auto-rejected applications at the organization level, and department leads have read-only visibility only for submitted applications interested in departments they lead. Organizers/Staff Coordinators may now approve submitted applications at the organization level, which changes the application to Approved, links/creates staff, creates/ensures Prospective organization status, records reviewer metadata, and writes audit history (M5.5). Organizers/Staff Coordinators may reject or defer submitted applications without creating staff access (M5.6). Applicants may withdraw only their own submitted applications from the public confirmation flow or when authenticated identity matches the application email (M5.6). Organizers/Staff Coordinators and department leads may now assign approved applicants to eligible event-participating departments, creating active department membership with the structural default team, promoting Prospective organization status to Active when no trainings are configured, and auditing the assignment (M5.7). Department leads may now assign staff with active department membership to additional teams within departments they lead, creating or reactivating `team_memberships` and auditing the assignment (M5.8). M5.9 adds organizer/Staff Coordinator rescind before operational team assignment, inactivates default-only department memberships and organization status, blocks after non-default team assignment history, and audits the rescind. M5.10 adds QA-APP-01 for the application approval, department assignment, team assignment, and rescind gate. Additional required profile/contact fields (section 5.2 step 4), dedicated API command surfaces, and richer assignment UI remain deferred. |

Staff-facing visibility remains with its owning M6/M11 tasks. | POL-003 | In progress | [M6.1](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M6.2](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M6.7](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `PolicyDocumentSchemaTest`; `ProcedureDocumentSchemaTest`; `DocumentOrchidTest` | [QA-POL-01](../qa/QA-POL-01-policy-procedure-fragment-acknowledgment.md) | Policy and procedure documents record organization, department, or team scope through documented `scope_type` values and matching UUID `scope_id` values. | POL-006; POL-008 through POL-010 | In progress | [M11.15](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M11.20](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M18.7](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `EventInfoHttpTest`; `DocumentProductHttpTest`; `PolicyDocumentSchemaTest`; `ProcedureDocumentSchemaTest`; `StaffEventInfoViews.spec.ts`; `StaffDocumentViews.spec.ts` | [QA-STAFF-02](../qa/QA-STAFF-02-staff-me-and-event-info.md); [QA-POL-02](../qa/QA-POL-02-product-document-authoring-sharing.md) | Published documents now reach a staff-facing product surface. `GET /api/events/{event}/info` and the `event.info` screen assemble published policy/procedure documents by Event Info section, resolving organization-scoped documents to every staff member in the organization, department-scoped documents to department members, and team-scoped documents to team members through the existing published-visibility rule. Event Info grants no visibility of its own and never shows draft or archived documents, including to their maintainer. M18.7 adds the library those documents were missing: `staff.documents` and `staff.document-detail` are neither department-scoped nor event-scoped, so a member of two departments reads every document published to them from one page instead of one department administration screen at a time. The list asks for `state=published`, which leaves a draft off the reading surface even for the maintainer who wrote it, and the detail read is the authorization as well as the content — a document outside the caller's visibility is the node's 403 rather than an empty page. PowerSync device delivery remains with its owning task. |
| POL-011; POL-012 | In progress | [M11.15](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M11.20](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M18.7](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `EventInfoHttpTest`; `DocumentProductHttpTest`; `StaffDocumentViews.spec.ts` | [QA-STAFF-02](../qa/QA-STAFF-02-staff-me-and-event-info.md); [QA-POL-02](../qa/QA-POL-02-product-document-authoring-sharing.md) | Department and team leads see the documents in their leadership scope through maintainer visibility, and roles holding `policies.view_published` see all published documents across the organization. Event Info reuses the same rule rather than adding one, so lead and organizer breadth on that screen matches the document library, and the M18.7 staff library reuses it a third time: a lead reads the documents published to them on the same personal page everybody else does, and reaches the ones they maintain from the Admin workflow where authoring lives. |
| POL-013 | Complete | [M11.15](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M18.6](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M18.7](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `DocumentProductHttpTest`; `DocumentAcknowledgmentHttpTest`; `StaffDocumentViews.spec.ts` | [QA-POL-02](../qa/QA-POL-02-product-document-authoring-sharing.md) | Every document read requires an authenticated caller, and no policy/procedure surface is reachable before login. The single exception the requirement allows is the signup path: `signup.policy-acknowledgment` shows a document to somebody completing staff signup, and it shows only documents an active acknowledgment requirement addressed to them. The staff library adds no public route — an address typed or forwarded for a document outside the caller's visibility is refused by the node rather than rendered empty. |
| POL-055 | Complete | [M18.7](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `DocumentProductHttpTest`; `StaffDocumentViews.spec.ts` | [QA-POL-02](../qa/QA-POL-02-product-document-authoring-sharing.md) | The document library read takes an optional `?q=` title search, applied after the caller's visibility has been decided and never before it, which is what the requirement asks for by qualifying searchability with visibility permissions: a matching title in a scope the caller is outside stays absent rather than becoming a way to learn that the document exists. Titles only, per data/API 11.12; case-insensitive substring; an empty term is not a filter. `staff.documents` puts the term in its URL and sends it to the node rather than narrowing the list the browser holds, so a search result someone links to or reloads is the same result. |
| Data/API spec: Section 11.4A Event Info section assembly | In progress | [M11.20](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M16.19](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `EventInfoHttpTest`; `DocumentProductHttpTest`; `PolicyDocumentSchemaTest`; `ProcedureDocumentSchemaTest`; `StaffEventInfoViews.spec.ts`; `DocumentViews.spec.ts` | [QA-STAFF-02](../qa/QA-STAFF-02-staff-me-and-event-info.md) | Policy and procedure documents carry a nullable `event_info_section` from the documented vocabulary (`directions`, `arrival`, `packing`, `food`, `housing`, `requirements`). Assignment is explicit maintainer placement rather than inference from titles or slugs, needs no capability beyond maintain-scope authority, and does not bump the document version because placement is not content. Assembly returns every section in documented order with published visible documents ordered organization scope first, then department, then team, then title, and an explicit `empty_description` where nothing is visible. M16.19 makes that assembly the only one: `event.info` renders `GET /api/events/{event}/info` — the node's section order, labels, rendered document HTML, and named gaps — and the browser's second copy of the rule, which held the six keys, the six empty descriptions, the published-only filter, and the scope-breadth sort over bundled documents, is gone. The section vocabulary the authoring form offers comes from `event_info_sections` on the document read for the same reason, so a placement the form proposes is one the command accepts. |

Authorization matches the Admin page, so department administer authority reaches every department team, team leads reach only teams they lead, and a named team the viewer may not open fails closed instead of substituting one they may. |
| UI Implementation Contract: Section 7.0 Primary navigation menus | In progress | [M11.20](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `AppShell.spec.ts`; `StaffPageTemplate.spec.ts` | [QA-STAFF-02](../qa/QA-STAFF-02-staff-me-and-event-info.md) | The shell carries a Staff menu holding Me plus Event Info whenever the interface is locked to an event, and a Workflows menu holding only hubs. Me is no longer a workflow. Below ten combined items the shell renders one menu instead of two, ordered staff pages then workflows, because a short list split across two dropdowns makes the reader guess which one holds the page. No Alpha 1 role reaches ten yet (a fully capable department lead reaches nine), so the split is a rule waiting on a role that needs it rather than behavior anyone currently sees. |
| UI Implementation Contract: Section 11.5A StaffPageShell and staff card lists | In progress | [M11.20](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `StaffPageTemplate.spec.ts`; `DocumentViews.spec.ts`; `FieldReportsAuthorViews.spec.ts` | [QA-STAFF-02](../qa/QA-STAFF-02-staff-me-and-event-info.md) | Staff-facing informational pages use a single measured mobile-first column with labelled cards and 44px targets instead of a table that scrolls its headers off screen. Applied to My Field Reports, Event Info, and the reader views of Documents, Shifts, and Trainings; the same pages keep the wide workflow shell and DataTable for viewers with lead authority, since the reader view is a different shape rather than the lead table with columns hidden. |
| UI Implementation Contract: Sections 10.2 Layout tokens and 11.5B ContentGrid and large-display scaling | In progress | [M11.20](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `ContentGrid.spec.ts`; `StaffPageTemplate.spec.ts` | [QA-STAFF-02](../qa/QA-STAFF-02-staff-me-and-event-info.md) | Page containers grow with the viewport instead of stopping at a fixed rem cap, and reading measure is bounded per block rather than by the container, so extra width buys visible records rather than longer sentences. ContentGrid tiles peer cards and sections with auto-fill (a two-record list stays two normal tiles rather than two stretched ones), while headings, toolbars, and tables stay full width between tiled groups. Beyond 2560px the root font size steps up so every rem-based token scales together for viewing distance; scaling deliberately starts above 1920px, which is more often a desk monitor than a wall. Applies to both page templates and to Home. Fitting a page on one screen at large sizes is the design target, not a guarantee: a long enough list still scrolls. |
| UI Implementation Contract: Sections 4.16-4.20 Global layout rules, 11.5C Density axis, 11.5D ControlBar and ControlField, 11.5E Page regions | In progress | [M11.20](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `ControlBar.spec.ts`; `WorkflowDensity.spec.ts`; `ContentGrid.spec.ts` | [QA-STAFF-02](../qa/QA-STAFF-02-staff-me-and-event-info.md) | Workflow pages re-flow instead of re-sizing. Density tokens tighten block padding and stack gaps as width grows while inline padding grows, so a band gets shorter and roomier together. Page headings share a baseline row from 90rem and lift summary cards beside the title from 120rem. ControlBar replaces stacked per-form bands with one band whose groups size to their content and where exactly one group absorbs leftover width; ControlField sizes a control by what it holds rather than by sibling count, and puts its label beside the control past a phone. ContentGrid `min="region"` lets a page declare peer blocks that sit side by side, using auto-fit rather than the auto-fill records use, because regions are an authored set while records are unbounded data. Measured at 1920x1080: incident list 1191px to 617px of page content, Planning 2394px to 1763px, Admin 2665px to 2184px, Department Overview 1433px to within one screen. Pairing is peer-only: a wide table beside a narrow panel measured taller and was reverted. |
| UI Implementation Contract: Sections 11.5F HeroCenterLayout and 4.21 Read-only administration defaults | In progress | [M11.20](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `StaffPageTemplate.spec.ts`; `DepartmentTeamViews.spec.ts` | [QA-STAFF-02](../qa/QA-STAFF-02-staff-me-and-event-info.md) | Event Info holds its summary in the middle of the page past ~1500px with its six section cards around it, using dense placement so the layout does not depend on card count; the summary is a peer of the cards rather than the page header, so nothing is duplicated against the `h1`. Admin department details now open read-only behind an Edit control with a Cancel that discards the draft, matching the team details panel beside them, because these are organization-visible identity fields and landing mid-form is not a safe default. Incident edit becomes two panel columns past ~1500px and moves the timeline beside the form past 120rem. |
| UI Implementation Contract: Section 16.1A Node connection scale | In progress | [M11.20](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `AppShell.spec.ts` | [QA-STAFF-02](../qa/QA-STAFF-02-staff-me-and-event-info.md) | The shell's user button and dropdown dot show one four-step scale of notice: unknown (`--m-text-muted`), failing (`--m-status-danger`), degraded (`--m-status-warning`), connected (`--m-status-success`). Previously the treatment was three undocumented tones blended roughly two thirds into `--m-text-muted`, which flattened the scale into the surrounding gray; the colours now render at full strength. Colour never carries the state alone — the canonical 16.1 label remains the accessible name and the dropdown text. Unknown is defined and unit tested but not reachable at runtime, because the current device-network signal resolves synchronously; it becomes reachable when a node-sync signal with an indeterminate period lands. |

| POL-023 through POL-027; POL-046; POL-047 | Complete | [M6.9](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M6.10](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M18.6](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `DocumentAcknowledgmentRequirementTest`; `DocumentAcknowledgmentServiceTest`; `DocumentAcknowledgmentHttpTest`; `PermissionCatalogTest`; `apps/client/src/views/DocumentAcknowledgmentViews.spec.ts` | [QA-POL-01](../qa/QA-POL-01-policy-procedure-fragment-acknowledgment.md) | M6.9 adds the UUID-backed `document_acknowledgment_requirements` model, factory, and validated domain creation path. It supports only organization/department scope and signup/training contexts, rejecting invalid scope targets and cross-organization documents. M6.10 accepts active requirements through the connected Laravel domain action; it does not add shift/credential gates, an API controller, acknowledgment UI, or offline writes. M18.6 gives both services their callers and the three surfaces UI contract 12.1, 12.3, and 12.6 name. Requirement administration is `documents.acknowledgments.review`, a new catalog capability held by the two organizer roles and no others, and it maintains only the organization and department scopes POL-047 permits and the signup and training contexts POL-046 permits — the create form offers exactly those and nothing else, sourced from the node. A requirement pointing at an unpublished document is refused at creation and absent from every read, and retiring one is a switch that leaves the acknowledgments recorded against it standing. POL-026 and POL-027 are asserted rather than assumed: `DocumentAcknowledgmentHttpTest` signs a staff member up for a shift and evaluates their credential with a requirement outstanding throughout, and the `/me` read carries a `gating` block stating both, because a list of outstanding items reads as a list of blockers unless the answer says otherwise. |
| POL-043 through POL-045 | Complete | [M6.10](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M18.6](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `DocumentAcknowledgmentServiceTest`; `DocumentAcknowledgmentHttpTest`; `apps/client/src/views/DocumentAcknowledgmentViews.spec.ts` | [QA-POL-01](../qa/QA-POL-01-policy-procedure-fragment-acknowledgment.md) | Connected acceptance creates an immutable `document_acknowledgments` row with policy/procedure ID, separate document/fragment revisions, organization/department requirement scope, timestamp, accepting node, optional linked staff profile, and an audit event. One immutable source/resolved-Markdown snapshot is retained per acknowledged version; acknowledgment rows store no rendered text. Repeating an unchanged acceptance is idempotent, and later document/fragment changes neither alter nor automatically re-require existing acknowledgment records. M18.6 makes all three readable rather than merely recorded: `POST /api/commands/acknowledge-document` records the version that was shown and answers with it, the document is rendered with its fragments inline on the surface that asks for the acceptance (POL-022), and a row whose document has since moved reports `document_changed_since` while staying answered — POL-045 in a field, so a reader is told rather than left wondering, and so no client re-derives an outstanding state the specification removed. A requirement the caller is outside the scope of is refused with 403 rather than merely hidden. |

| Technical spec: Section 21 Policies, Procedures, and Fragments | In progress | [M6.1](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) through [M6.12](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M11.15](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M12.7](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `PolicyDocumentSchemaTest`; `ProcedureDocumentSchemaTest`; `DocumentFragmentSchemaTest`; `DocumentFragmentReferenceServiceTest`; `DocumentRendererTest`; `DocumentFragmentVersionBumpJobTest`; `DocumentOrchidTest`; `DocumentAcknowledgmentRequirementTest`; `DocumentAcknowledgmentServiceTest`; `DocumentExportServiceTest`; `DocumentProductHttpTest`; `DocumentViews.spec.ts`; `GovernanceEditFreezeTest` | [QA-POL-01](../qa/QA-POL-01-policy-procedure-fragment-acknowledgment.md); [QA-POL-02](../qa/QA-POL-02-product-document-authoring-sharing.md) | M6.1-M6.6 establish separate Markdown policy/procedure models, reusable fragments, validated references, a reusable sanitized rendered viewer, and retry-safe queued published-document fragment revision bumps. M6.7 adds audited Orchid document/fragment administration, title filtering, scope/state/version display, saved inline previews, and reference visibility. M6.8 adds an accessible pre-save Orchid warning with the exact published-reference impact count. M6.9 adds organization/department signup/training acknowledgment requirement configuration. M6.10 adds connected, idempotent acknowledgment acceptance with required version snapshots and audit history. M6.11 adds audited single-document Markdown/PDF downloads with resolved fragments and required export metadata. M6.12 adds the end-to-end human QA scenario. M11.15 moves MVP maintainer creation/sharing workflows into normal Meridian Admin and department/team product surfaces with product API authorization, visibility review, fragment-impact review, and export/share entry points. M12.7 implements section 21.10's active-window rule: document and fragment edits, and the queued fragment revision bump, are refused on every node while any event of the owning organization is in its active window, while acknowledgments, version snapshots, and acknowledgment requirements stay writable (see the Section 10.2 Event authority row). M18.6 adds the acknowledgment screens of UI contract 12.1, 12.3, and 12.6 and the API path behind them. PowerSync and packet assembly remain with their owning work. |
| Technical spec: Section 22.2 Alpha 1 Orchid screens | In progress | [M6.7](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M13.7](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M13.8](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `DocumentOrchidTest`; `ImportOrchidTest`; `UserImportTest`; `TeamImportTest`; `ShiftImportTest`; `AssignmentImportTest` | [QA-POL-01](../qa/QA-POL-01-policy-procedure-fragment-acknowledgment.md); [QA-IMPORT-01](../qa/QA-IMPORT-01-users-teams-import.md) | Separate Policy Documents, Procedure Documents, and Document Fragments list/create/edit screens are now registered and permission-gated in Orchid. Document acknowledgment screens remain blocked on their owning acknowledgment-model/action tasks. M13.7 adds the first of the section's CSV imports: God-mode **Import Users** and **Import Teams** screens gated by the new `platform.imports` console permission, each accepting an uploaded or pasted CSV. `CsvImportReader` normalizes header names, ignores unknown columns and column order, and rejects a file missing a required column whole, while a bad row is skipped with a reason so one line never aborts the import. Users carry `email` and `name` only — never passwords, console permissions, roles, or the disabled flag — and an imported account has no console access and no verified email, so a spreadsheet cannot grant anyone anything. Teams name their department by organization slug plus department code and are written through `TeamAdminService`, so an imported team is validated and audited exactly like a hand-created one. Rows match existing records (users by normalized email, teams by department and team code), so re-running a corrected file updates instead of duplicating, and nothing is ever deleted, archived, or disabled by an import. **Preview** is the real import inside a rolled-back transaction, so what an operator is shown cannot drift from what importing does. Each run records a `users.imported`/`teams.imported` summary alongside the per-record entries. M13.8 adds the section's remaining two imports: **Import Shifts** and **Import Assignments**, on the same `platform.imports` permission and the same preview/per-row-outcome behavior. Shift rows name their event, department, and eligible team by slug and code, and are written through `ShiftAdminService`, so an imported shift is validated and audited exactly like one created on the shift screen. A time without an offset is read in the event's timezone and stored as UTC; a value carrying `Z` or an offset is honored as written. A shift is matched by event, department, eligible team, title, and start together, so a corrected capacity or end time updates it while a retitled or moved row creates a new shift; an optional column the file omits leaves the existing value alone, a present-but-empty one clears it, and training and waiver requirements the file cannot express are carried through unchanged. No import cancels a shift. Assignment rows name a staff member by email and the shift by title and start, with `team_code` disambiguating the only case a title and start can match twice, and are written through `ShiftAssignmentService::assignStaffToShiftFromImport`, which shares one implementation with lead assignment: authority is the console permission rather than a department lead role and the signup window is not a gate, because a roster arrives as a spreadsheet after signup closes, while Do Not Staff, department membership, eligible team membership, Ineligible status, and required trainings and waivers all still refuse the row with the domain's own reason. Every imported assignment is recorded as lead-assigned by the operator, so the roster export's `assignment_source` (REPORT-002) cannot be falsified from a file, and nobody is removed from a shift by an import. CSV export from these screens remains with its owning task. |

| GOD-012 through GOD-017 | Implemented | [M15B.6](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M15B.7](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `ConsoleDocumentationTest` | [QA-GOD-01](../qa/QA-GOD-01-console-orientation-docs-changelog.md) | `docs/technician/` covers deployment, node setup and pairing, configuration and config source resolution, data repair, sync conflict resolution, and break-glass procedures. `corepack pnpm run docs:package` copies that tree into `apps/server/resources/technician-docs` with a manifest recording the packaged version; `docs:check` runs in `process:check` so the packaged copy cannot drift. | GOD-018 through GOD-026 | Implemented | [M15B.8](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M15B.9](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M15B.10](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `ConsoleChangelogTest` | [QA-GOD-01](../qa/QA-GOD-01-console-orientation-docs-changelog.md) | `corepack pnpm run changelog:generate` derives entries from first-parent history, assigns each pull request the version of the bump that followed it, and writes a gitignored data file the release build packages. Refresh runs only on central, only outside the active event window, and only after the response is sent, so rendering never waits. Missing network, missing credential, and failure all degrade to the packaged baseline with the last successful refresh time. Failure reasons are fixed strings rather than exception messages, because a client exception can carry the credential. |
| GOD-027, GOD-028; Technical spec: Section 22.1 Orchid purpose; Section 22.5-22.8 God Mode console; Section 26.3 Versioning | Implemented | [M15B.1](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M15B.11](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `ConsoleDocumentationTest` | [QA-GOD-01](../qa/QA-GOD-01-console-orientation-docs-changelog.md) | Technical spec section 22 gained 22.5 landing screen, 22.6 technician documentation, 22.7 changelog, and 22.8 external links and version display. The external Orchid documentation and changelog menu entries are replaced by internal console pages, and the navigation badge is the Meridian build version. |
| GOD-029, GOD-034 through GOD-036 | Implemented | [M15C.1](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M15C.2](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M15C.7](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M15C.9](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `ConsoleVisualIdentityTest`, `ConsoleContrastTest` | [QA-GOD-02](../qa/QA-GOD-02-console-visual-identity.md) | `public/css/meridian-console.css` maps the framework's Bootstrap custom properties onto the shared `--m-*` tokens for surface, foreground, border, focus, action, typography, spacing, radius, and elevation, and restates no color of its own — a test fails on any hex literal in the bridge. The console is Meridian's palette permanently: it links no organization branding stylesheet and carries no `data-organization-branding` attribute, and a test compares the rendered styling of the console with and without a branded organization active. `ConsoleContrastTest` resolves the token expressions the way a browser would, including `color-mix(in srgb, …)`, and measures every console pair in both themes against the same WCAG thresholds the branding validator applies. Two pairs needed console-specific derivations: control boundaries, because the dark theme's `--m-border-strong` literal measures 2.17:1 against the dark surface, and status *text*, because a status color is validated as a fill and as an indicator but never as a label. |
| GOD-030, GOD-031 | Implemented | [M15C.3](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md), [M15C.4](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `ConsoleVisualIdentityTest` | [QA-GOD-02](../qa/QA-GOD-02-console-visual-identity.md) | The brand lockup is the Meridian mark plus the product name, shared by the console navigation, the framework's authentication surface, and the standalone login/magic-link/logout/setup pages. The name is text rather than the raster wordmark because it has to read on the primary-colored navigation as well as on light surfaces. Collapsed navigation renders the mark alone, hiding the name visually rather than removing it so the brand link keeps its accessible name. The favicon is served from the console header hook, which is where the framework pushed its own. |
| GOD-032, GOD-033 | Implemented | [M15C.5](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `ConsoleVisualIdentityTest` | [QA-GOD-02](../qa/QA-GOD-02-console-visual-identity.md) | The footer states the license from the repository's root manifest through `config('meridian.license')` rather than a string in a template, so it cannot claim a license the repository does not carry; a test checks the manifest, the config value, and the `LICENSE` file agree on AGPL-3.0-or-later. The copyright range starts at 2026 and resolves "present" to the current year. The framework's MIT statement, its 2016-to-present range, its version link, its author credit, and its five project links are all gone, including from the guest footer on the framework's own sign-in page. |
| GOD-037 | Implemented | [M15C.8](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `ConsoleUpgradeSafetyTest` | [QA-GOD-02](../qa/QA-GOD-02-console-visual-identity.md) | The identity is applied entirely through `platform.resource.stylesheets`, `platform.template.header`, and `platform.template.footer`. No vendor view is published and no vendor asset is patched, so a framework upgrade does not require reapplying it. [The override inventory](god-mode-console-override-inventory.md) records the extension points in use and is enforced: a Blade file appearing under `resources/views/vendor/platform/` fails the test until it is listed there with a reason. The one place the framework's markup could not be changed — `data-bs-theme="dark"` hard-coded on the navigation column — is handled by styling that column as a primary-colored surface whose foreground is identical in both themes, rather than by overriding the view. |
| GOD-038 | Implemented | [M15C.6](meridian-alpha-1-development-plan.md) |  | `ConsoleVisualIdentityTest` | [QA-GOD-02](../qa/QA-GOD-02-console-visual-identity.md) | Login, the magic-link landing, sign-out, and node first-run setup share `meridian.surface-head`, `meridian.brand`, `meridian.footer`, and `public/css/meridian-surface.css`. Setup had been the only styled one of the four and carried its own inline copy of the token mapping; that copy is gone, and a test asserts the view has no `<style>` block, because a private copy is how these four drift into four identities. |
